\mathcal{L}_{-}(a_{\ell}^{k})=\log(3|k|)\leq 2\pi\lambda_{e^{(1)},e^{(2)}}

and
\mathcal{L}_{-}(a_{\ell^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})=\log(3|k^{\prime}|)\leq 2\pi\lambda_{e^{(1)},e^{(2)}}
.

If two of the elements
f_{*}(e^{(1)})
,
f_{*}(e^{(2)})
, and
f_{*}(e^{(1)}\,e^{(2)})=f_{*}(e^{(1)})\,f_{*}(e^{(2)})
, are not powers of a standard generator, then the
\mathcal{L}_{-}
of each of the two elements does not exceed
2\pi\lambda_{e^{(1)},e^{(2)}}
. Since the
\mathcal{L}_{-}
of an element coincides with the
\mathcal{L}_{-}
of its inverse, the third element is the product of two elements with
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding
2\pi\lambda_{e^{(1)},e^{(2)}}
.
Since for
x,x^{\prime}\geq 2
the inequality
\log(x+x^{\prime})\leq\log x+\log x^{\prime}
holds,
the
\mathcal{L}_{-}
of the product does not exceed the sum of the
\mathcal{L}_{-}
of the factors. Hence the
\mathcal{L}_{-}
of the third element does not exceed
4\pi\lambda_{e^{(1)},e^{(2)}}
. Hence, inequality (
15
) holds.

\Box

The following proposition states
the existence of suitable connected components of the zero set of the imaginary part of certain analytic functions on tori with a hole and on planar domains. For any subset
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
of
\pi_{1}(X;q_{0})
we denote by
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})^{-1}
the set of all elements that are inverse to elements in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
. Recall that
\mathcal{E}_{j}
is the set of primitive elements of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
which can be written as product of at most
j
elements of
\mathcal{E}\cup(\mathcal{E})^{-1}
for the set
\mathcal{E}
of generators of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
chosen in the introduction.

Proposition 3
.

Let
X
be a torus with a hole or a planar domain with base point
q_{0}
and fundamental group
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
, and let
\mathcal{E}
be a set of generators of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
that is associated to a standard bouquet of circles for
X
.
Let
f:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
be a non-contractible holomorphic mapping
such that
0
is a regular value of
{\rm{Im}}f
. Then there exist a simple relatively closed curve
L_{0}\subset X
such that

f(L_{0})\subset\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1,1\},
and a set
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}_{2}\subset\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
of primitive elements of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
,
such that the following holds.
Each element
e_{j,0}\in\mathcal{E}\subset\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
is the product of at most two elements of
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}\cup(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})^{-1}
. Moreover, for each
e_{0}\in\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
which is the product of one or two elements from
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}
the free homotopy class
\widehat{e_{0}}
has positive intersection number with
L_{0}
(after suitable orientation of
L_{0}
).

If
X
is a torus with a hole or
X
equals
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with three holes, we may chose
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}
consisting of two elements, one of them contained in
\mathcal{E}
, the other is either contained in
\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{E}^{-1}
or is a product of two elements of
\mathcal{E}
.

Notice the following facts. By Theorem E a mapping
f:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
is contractible if and only if for each
e_{0}\in\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
the monodromy
f_{*}(e_{0})
is equal to the identity. The mapping
f
is reducible if and only if the mondromy mapping
f_{*}:\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})\to\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},f(q_{0}))
is conjugate to a mapping into a subgroup
\Gamma
of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},f(q_{0}))
that is generated by a single element that is represented by a curve which separates one of the points
1,-1
or
\infty
from the other points. In other words,
\Gamma
is (after identifying fundamental groups with different base point up to conjugacy) generated by a conjugate of one of the elements
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
or
a_{1}a_{2}
of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},0)
.

If
f
is irreducible, then it is not contractible, and, hence, the preimage
f^{-1}(\mathbb{R})
is not empty.

Denote by
M_{1}
a Möbius transformation which permutes the points
-1,\,1,\,\infty
and maps the interval
(-\infty,-1)
onto
(-1,1)
, and let
M_{2}
be a Möbius transformation which permutes the points
-1,\,1,\,\infty
and maps the interval
(1,\infty)
onto
(-1,1)
. Let
M_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\mbox{Id}
.

The main step for the proof of Theorem
1
is the following Proposition
4
.

Recall that
\lambda_{j}(X)
was defined in the introduction. Since for
e_{0}\in\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})

the equality
\lambda(\tilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(\langle e_{0}\rangle))=\lambda(A(\widehat{e_{0}}))
holds,
\lambda_{j}(X)
is the maximum of
\lambda(A(\widehat{e_{0}}))
over
e_{0}\in\mathcal{E}_{j}
.

Proposition 4
.

Let
X
be a connected finite open Riemann surface with base point
q_{0}
, and let
\mathcal{E}
be the set of generators of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
that was chosen in Section 1.
Suppose

f:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
is an irreducible holomorphic mapping, such that

0
is a regular value of
{\rm{Im}}f
. Then for one of the functions
M_{l}\circ f,\,l=0,1,2,\,

which we denote by
F
, there exists a point
q\in X
(depending on
f
), such that
the point

q^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}F(q)
is contained in
(-1,1)
, and a curve
\alpha
in
X
joining
q_{0}
with
q
, such that the following holds.
For each element
e_{j}\in\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E})

the monodromy
F_{*}(e_{j})
is the product of at most four elements of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})
of
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding
2\pi\lambda_{7}(X)
and, hence,

(16)

\mathcal{L}_{-}(F_{*}(e_{j}))\leq 8\pi\lambda_{7}(X)\;\;\mbox{for each}\;\,j.

If
X
is a torus with a hole the proposition holds with
\lambda_{7}(X)
replaced by
\lambda_{3}(X)
. If
X
is a planar domain the proposition holds with
\lambda_{4}(X)
instead of
\lambda_{7}(X)
.

Notice, that
all monodromies of contractible mappings are equal to the identity, hence the inequality (
16
) holds automatically for contractible mappings.

We postpone the proof of the two propositions and prove first the Theorem
1
.

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Let
X
be a connected finite open Riemann surface (possibly of second kind)
with base point
q_{0}
. Consider an arbitrary open Riemann surface
X^{0}\Subset X
which is relatively compact in
X
and is a deformation retract of
X
.
Consider a free homotopy class of mappings from
X
to
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
, that is represented by an irreducible holomorphic mapping
{\sf f}:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
. Notice that the restriction
{\sf f}|X^{0}
is also irreducible.
Take a small enough positive number
\varepsilon
,
such that the function
({\sf f}-i\varepsilon)\mid X^{0}
takes values in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
and
0
is a regular value of its imaginary part. Put
f=({\sf f}-i\varepsilon)\mid X^{0}
. If
\varepsilon
is small enough, then the irreducible mapping
f
on
X^{0}
is free homotopic to
{\sf f}\mid X^{0}
.
We identify the fundamental groups of
X
and of
X^{0}
by the inclusion mapping from
X^{0}
to
X
.

Proposition
4
applied to the mapping
f:X^{0}\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}

provides a Möbius transformation
M_{l}
, that maps one of the components of
\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1,1\}
onto
(-1,1)
, and further a point
q\in X^{0}
and a curve
\alpha
in
X^{0}
with initial point
q_{0}
and terminating point
q
, such for the mapping

F=M_{l}\circ f
the point
q^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}F(q)
is contained in
(-1,1)
, and for the generators
e_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}{\rm Is}_{\alpha}(e_{j,0}),\;e_{j,0}\in\mathcal{E},
of
\pi_{1}(X^{0},q)
the inequalities (
16
) hold.
After identifying the fundamental groups
\pi_{1}(X,q)
and
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
with different base point by an isomorphism that is defined up to conjugation, Theorem E states that the free homotopy class of
F
corresponds to a conjugacy class of homomorphisms

\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})\cong\pi_{1}(X,q)\to\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})\,,

that is represented by a homomorphism
h
for which
\mathcal{L}_{-}(h(e_{j,0}))\leq 8\pi\lambda_{7}(X^{0})
for each
e_{j,0}\in\mathcal{E}
.
More explicitly, there exists
a smooth mapping
\tilde{F}:X^{0}\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
, that is free homotopic to
F
, maps
q_{0}
to
q^{\prime}
, and satisfies the inequality

(17)

\mathcal{L}_{-}(\tilde{F}_{*}(e_{j,0}))\leq 8\pi\lambda_{7}(X^{0})

for each
e_{j,0}\in\mathcal{E}
.
The existence of the smooth mapping
\tilde{F}
can be seen explicitly as follows.
Write
e=\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(e_{0})\in\pi_{1}(X,q)
for each
e_{0}\in\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
.
Parameterise
\alpha
by the interval
[0,1]
.
The image of
\alpha
under the mapping
F
is the curve
\beta=F\circ\alpha
in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
with initial point
F(q_{0})
and terminating point
F(q)=q^{\prime}
. Then
F_{*}(e_{0})=(\mbox{Is}_{\beta})^{-1}(F_{*}(e))
.
Choose a homotopy
F_{t},\,t\in[0,1]
, that joins the mapping
F_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}F
with a (smooth) mapping
F_{1}
denoted by
\tilde{F}
, so that
F_{t}(q_{0})=\beta(t),\,t\in[0,1]
.
The value
\beta(t)

moves from the point
\beta(0)=F(q_{0})
to
\beta(1)=q^{\prime}
along the curve
\beta
.
Then
\tilde{F}(q_{0})=q^{\prime}
and
\tilde{F}_{*}(e_{0})=F_{*}(e)
for each
e_{0}\in\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
.
Indeed,
denote by
\beta_{t}
the curve that runs from
\beta(t)
to
\beta(1)
along
\beta
. Then
\beta_{0}=\beta
and
\beta_{1}
is a constant curve.
Let
\gamma_{0}
be a curve that represents
e_{0}
.
The base point of the curve
F_{t}(\gamma_{0})
equals
F_{t}(q_{0})=\beta(t)
. Hence, we obtain a continuous family of curves
\beta_{t}^{-1}F_{t}(\gamma_{0})\beta_{t}
with base point
\beta(1)=F(q)
. For
t=1
the curve is equal to
F_{1}(\gamma_{0})=\tilde{F}(\gamma_{0})
, for
t=0
the curve is equal to
\beta^{-1}F_{0}(\gamma_{0})\beta=F_{0}(\alpha^{-1}{\rm Is}_{\alpha}(\gamma_{0})\alpha)=F_{0}(\gamma)
. Since the two curves

F_{1}(\gamma_{0})
and
F_{0}(\gamma)

are homotopic and
F_{1}=\tilde{F}
,
F_{0}=F
, we obtain
\tilde{F}_{*}(e_{0})=F_{*}(e)
.
The inequalities (
16
)
imply the inequalities
(
17
).

For each irreducible holomorphic mapping
{\sf f}:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
we found a Möbius transformation
M_{l}
and a mapping
\tilde{F}:X^{0}\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
that satisfies the condition
{\tilde{F}}(q_{0})=q^{\prime}\in(-1,1)
and the inequalities (
17
), and is free homotopic on
X^{0}
to
M_{l}(({\sf f}-i\varepsilon)|X^{0})
for a small number
\varepsilon
, and, hence, is free homotopic to

M_{l}({\sf f}|X^{0})
. Using a deformation retraction we obtain a mapping
\tilde{F}^{X}:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
that is free homotopic on
X^{0}
to
\tilde{F}
and, hence, to
M_{l}({\sf f}|X^{0})
. Identifying the fundamental groups
\pi_{1}(X^{0},q_{0})
and
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
by the homomorphism induced by the inclusion and applying Theorem E, we obtain for each irreducible holomorphic mapping
{\sf f}:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
a Möbius transformation
M_{l}
and
a smooth mapping
\tilde{F}^{X}:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
that is free homotopic to
M_{l}(\sf{f})
on
X
, and satisfies the condition
{\tilde{F}}^{X}(q_{0})=q^{\prime}\in(-1,1)
and the inequalities (
17
).

If
{\sf f}:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
is a contractible mapping, it is free homotopic to the function
\tilde{F}^{X}\equiv 0
on
X
, and the inequalities (
17
) are automatically satisfied for the monodromies of
\tilde{F}^{X}
.

The number of free homotopy classes of mappings
X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
, that contain a smooth mapping
\tilde{F}^{X}
, which satisfies the condition
\tilde{F}^{X}(q_{0})=q^{\prime}\in(-1,1)
and
the inequalities (
17
), are estimated from above as follows.
By Lemma 1 of
[
19
]
there are at most

\frac{1}{2}e^{24\pi\lambda_{7}(X^{0})}+1\leq\frac{3}{2}e^{24\pi\lambda_{7}(X^{0})}

different reduced words
w\in\pi_{1}(\mathcal{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}),0)
(including the identity) with
\mathcal{L}_{-}(w)\leq 8\pi\lambda_{7}(X^{0})
.
Identify standard generators of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})
with standard generators of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},0)

by the canonical isomorphism.
We saw, that there are at most
(\frac{3}{2}e^{24\pi\lambda_{7}(X^{0})})^{2g+m}
different homomorphisms
h:\pi_{1}(X^{0},q_{0})\cong\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})\to\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})\cong\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},0)
with
\mathcal{L}_{-}(h(e))\leq 8\pi\lambda_{7}(X^{0})
for each element
e
of the set of generators
\mathcal{E}
of
\pi_{1}(X^{0},q_{0})
.
By Theorem E there are at most
(\frac{3}{2}e^{24\pi\lambda_{7}(X^{0})})^{2g+m}
different
free homotopy classes of mappings
X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
, that contain a smooth mapping
\tilde{F}^{X}
which satisfies the condition
\tilde{F}^{X}(q_{0})=q^{\prime}\in(-1,1)
and
the inequalities (
17
).

For each irreducible or contractible mapping
\sf{f}
on
X
one of the mappings
M_{l}\circ\sf{f}
,
l=0,1,2,

is free homotopic to a mapping
\tilde{F}^{X}
which satisfies the condition
\tilde{F}^{X}(q_{0})=q^{\prime}\in(-1,1)
and
the inequalities (
17
). The
(M_{l})^{-1}\circ\tilde{F}^{X}
represent at most

3(\frac{3}{2}e^{24\pi\lambda_{7}(X^{0})})^{2g+m}
free homotopy classes of irreducible or contractible mappings
X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
.
Theorem 1 is proved with the upper bound
3(\frac{3}{2}e^{24\pi\lambda_{7}(X^{0})})^{2g+m}
for an arbitrary relatively compact domain
X^{0}\subset X
that is a deformation retract of
X
.

It remains to prove that
\lambda_{7}(X)=\inf\{\lambda_{7}(X^{0}):X^{0}\Subset X\;\mbox{is\, a\, deformation\, retract\, of\,}X\,\}
.
We have to prove that for each
e_{0}\in\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
the quantity
\lambda(A(\widehat{e_{0}}))=\lambda(\widetilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(\langle e_{0}\rangle))
is
equal to the infimum of
\lambda(\widetilde{X^{0}}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(\langle e_{0}\rangle))
over all
X^{0}
being open relatively compact subsets of
X
which are deformation retracts of
X
. Here
\widetilde{X^{0}}
is the universal covering of
X^{0}
, and the fundamental groups of
X
and
X^{0}
are identified.
\widetilde{X^{0}}
(
\widetilde{X}
, respectively) can be defined as set of homotopy classes of arcs in
X^{0}
(in
X
, respectively) joining
q_{0}
with a point
q\in X^{0}
(in
X
respectively) equipped with the complex structure induced by the projection to the endpoint of the arcs, and the point
\tilde{q}_{0}
corresponds to the class of the constant curve.
The isomorphism
({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}
from
\pi_{1}(X^{0},q_{0})
to the group of covering transformations on
\widetilde{X^{0}}
is defined in the same way as it was done for
X
instead of
X^{0}
.
These considerations imply that there is a holomorphic mapping from
\widetilde{X}^{0}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(\langle e_{0}\rangle)
into
\widetilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(\langle e_{0}\rangle)
. Hence, the extremal length of the first set is not smaller than the extremal length of the second set.

Vice versa, take any annulus
A^{0}
which is a relatively compact subset of
A(\widehat{e_{0}})
and is a deformation retract of
A(\widehat{e_{0}})
. Its projection to
X
is relatively compact in
X
, hence, it is contained in a relatively compact deformation retract
X^{0}
of
X
. Hence,
A^{0}
can be considered as subset of

\widetilde{X^{0}}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(\langle e_{0}\rangle)
, and, hence,
\lambda(\widetilde{X^{0}}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(\langle e_{0}\rangle))\leq\lambda(A^{0})
. Since
\lambda(A(\widehat{e_{0}}))=\inf\{\lambda(A^{0}):A^{0}\Subset A(\widehat{e_{0}})\mbox{\,is\, a\, deformation\, retract\, of\,}A(\widehat{e_{0}})\}
we are done.
\Box

We proved a slightly stronger statement, namely, the number of homotopy classes of mappings
X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
that contain a contractible holomorphic mapping or an irreducible holomorphic mapping does not exceed
(\frac{3}{2}e^{24\pi\lambda_{7}(X)})^{2g+m}
.

Proof of Proposition
3
.

Denote the zero set
\{x\in X:\mbox{Im}f(x)=0\}
by
L
. Since
f
is not contractible,
L\neq\emptyset
.

1. A torus with a hole.
Assume first that
X
is a torus with a hole with base point
q_{0}
.
For notational convenience we denote by
e_{0}
and
{e^{\prime}_{0}}
the two elements of the set of generators
\mathcal{E}
of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
that is associated to a standard bouquet of circles for
X
.
We claim that there is a connected component
L_{0}
of
L
such that (after suitable orientation) the intersection number of the free homotopy class of one of the elements of
\mathcal{E}
, say of
\widehat{e_{0}}
with
L_{0}
is positive, and
the intersection number with one of the classes

e^{\prime}_{0}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

, or

(e^{\prime}_{0})^{-1}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

, or

e_{0}\,e^{\prime}_{0}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

with
L_{0}
is positive.

The claim is easy to prove in the case when there is a component of
L_{0}
which is a simple closed curve that is not contractible and not contractible to the hole of
X
. Indeed, consider the inclusion of
X
into a closed torus
X^{c}
and the homomorphism on fundamental groups
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})\to\pi_{1}(X^{c},q_{0})
induced by the inclusion. Denote by
e_{0}^{c}
and
{e^{\prime}_{0}}^{c}
the images of
e_{0}
and
e_{0}^{\prime}
under this homomorphism. Notice that
e_{0}^{c}
and
{e^{\prime}_{0}}^{c}
commute. The (image under the inclusion of the) curve
L_{0}
is a simple closed non-contractible curve in
X^{c}
. It represents the free homotopy class of an element
(e_{0}^{c})^{j}({e^{\prime}_{0}}^{c})^{k}
for some integers
j
and
k
which are not both equal to zero. Hence,
L_{0}
is not null-homologous in
X^{c}
, and by the Poincaré Duality Theorem
for one of the generators, say for
e_{0}^{c}
, the representatives of the free homotopy class

e_{0}^{c}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

have non-zero intersection number with
L_{0}
.
After suitable orientation of
L_{0}
, we may assume that this intersection number is positive. There is a representative of the class

e_{0}^{c}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

which is contained in
X
, hence,

\widehat{e_{0}}
has positive intersection number with
L_{0}
.

Suppose all compact connected components of
L
are contractible or
contractible to the hole of
X
.
Consider a relatively compact domain
{X}^{\prime\prime}\Subset{X}
in
{X}
with smooth boundary which is a deformation retract of
{X}
such that for each connected component of
L
at most one component of its intersection with
{X}^{\prime\prime}
is not contractible to the hole of
{X}^{\prime\prime}
. (See the paragraph on ”Regular zero sets”.) There is at least one component of
L\cap{X}^{\prime\prime}
that is not contractible to the hole of
{X}^{\prime\prime}
. Indeed, otherwise the free homotopy class of each element of
\mathcal{E}
could be represented by a loop avoiding
L
, and, hence, the monodromy of
f
along each
element of
\mathcal{E}
would be conjugate to the identity, and, hence, equal to the identity, i.e. contrary to the assumption,
f:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
would be free homotopic to a constant.

Take a component
L_{0}^{\prime\prime}
of
L\cap{X}^{\prime\prime}
that is not contractible to the hole of
{X}^{\prime\prime}
.
There is an arc of
\partial{X}^{\prime\prime}
between the endpoints of
L_{0}^{\prime\prime}
such that
the union
\tilde{L}_{0}
of the component
L_{0}^{\prime\prime}
with this arc is a closed curve in
{X}
that is
not contractible and not contractible to the hole. Hence, for one of the elements of
\mathcal{E}
, say for
e_{0}
, the intersection number of the free homotopy class
\widehat{e_{0}}
with the closed curve
\tilde{L}_{0}
is positive after orienting the curve
\tilde{L}_{0}
suitably. We may take a representative
\gamma
of
\widehat{e_{0}}
that is
contained in
{X}^{\prime\prime}
. Then
\gamma
has positive intersection number with
L_{0}^{\prime\prime}
.
Denote the connected component of
L
that contains
L_{0}^{\prime\prime}
by
L_{0}
. All components of
L_{0}\cap{X}^{\prime\prime}
different from
L_{0}^{\prime\prime}
are contractible to the hole of
{X}^{\prime\prime}
. Hence,
\gamma
has intersection number zero with each of these components.
Hence,
\gamma
has positive intersection number with
L_{0}
since
{\gamma}\subset{X}^{\prime\prime}
.
We proved that the class
\widehat{e_{0}}

has positive intersection number with
L_{0}
.

If

e^{\prime}_{0}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

also has non-zero intersection number with
L_{0}
we define
e^{\prime\prime}_{0}=(e^{\prime}_{0})^{\pm 1}
so that the intersection number of

e^{\prime\prime}_{0}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

with
L_{0}
is positive. If

e^{\prime}_{0}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

has zero intersection number with
L_{0}
we put
e^{\prime\prime}_{0}=e_{0}\,e^{\prime}_{0}
. Then again the intersection number of

e^{\prime\prime}_{0}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

with
L_{0}
is positive. Also, the intersection number of

e_{0}\,e^{\prime\prime}_{0}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

with
L_{0}
is positive. The set
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\{e_{0},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\}
satisfies the condition required in the proposition.
We obtained Proposition
3
for a torus with a hole.

2. A planar domain.
Let
X
be a planar domain.
The domain
X
is conformally equivalent to a disc with
m
smoothly bounded holes,
equivalently, to the Riemann sphere with
m+1
smoothly bounded holes,
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\bigcup_{j=1}^{m+1}\mathcal{C}_{j}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{m+1}
contains the point
\infty
. As before the base point of
X
is denoted by
q_{0}
, and for each
j=1,\ldots,m,
the generator
e_{j,0}\in\mathcal{E}\subset\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})

is represented by a curve that surrounds
\mathcal{C}_{j}
once counterclockwise.
Since
f
is not contractible, there must be a connected component of
L
that has limit points on some
\mathcal{C}_{j}
with
j\leq m
. Indeed, otherwise
the free homotopy class of each generator could be represented by a curve that avoids
L
.
This would imply that all monodromies are equal to the identity.
We claim that
there exists a component
L_{0}
of
L

with limit points on the boundary of two components
\partial{\mathcal{C}}_{j^{\prime}}
and

\partial{\mathcal{C}}_{j^{\prime\prime}}
for some
j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}\in\{1,\ldots,m+1\}
with
j^{\prime\prime}\neq j^{\prime}
.

Indeed, assume the contrary.
Then, if a component of
L
has limit points on a component
\partial{\mathcal{C}}_{j},\,j\leq m,
then all its limit points are on
\partial{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
.
Take a smoothly bounded simply connected domain
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{j}\Subset X\cup{{\mathcal{C}}_{j}}

that contains the closed set
{{\mathcal{C}}_{j}}
, so that its boundary
\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{j}
represents

e_{j,0}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

.
Then all components
L^{\prime}_{k}
of
L\setminus{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{j}
with an endpoint on
\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}
have another endpoint on this circle. The two endpoints of
L_{k}^{\prime}
on
\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{j}
divide
\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{j}
into two connected components.
The union of
\overline{L_{k}^{\prime}}
with each of the two components of
\partial{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus\overline{L_{k}^{\prime}}
is a simple closed curve in
\mathbb{C}
, and, hence, by the Jordan Curve Theorem it bounds a relatively compact topological disc in
\mathbb{C}
. One of these discs contains
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{j}
, the other does not.
Assign to each component
L_{k}^{\prime}
of
L\setminus{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{j}
with both endpoints on
\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}
the closed arc
\alpha_{k}
in
\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}
with the same endpoints as
L_{k}^{\prime}
, whose union with
L_{k}^{\prime}
bounds a relatively compact topological disc in
\mathbb{C}
that does not contain
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}
. These discs are partially ordered by inclusion,
since the
L_{k}^{\prime}
are pairwise disjoint. Hence, the arcs
\alpha_{k}
are partially ordered by inclusion. For an arc
\alpha_{k}
which contains no other of the arcs (a minimal arc) the curve
f\circ\alpha_{k}
except its endpoints is contained in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
. Moreover,
the endpoints of
f\circ\alpha_{k}
lie on
{f(\overline{L_{k}^{\prime}})}
, which is contained in one connected components of
\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1,1\}
, since
\overline{L_{k}^{\prime}}
is connected. Hence, the curve
f\circ\alpha_{k}
is homotopic in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
(with fixed endpoints) to a curve in
\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1,1\}
. The function
f
either maps all points on
\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}\setminus\alpha_{k}
that are close to
\alpha_{k}
to the open upper half-plane or maps them all to the open lower half-plane. (Recall, that zero is a regular value of
\mbox{Im}f
.) Hence, for an open arc
\alpha^{\prime}_{k}\subset\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}
that contains
\alpha_{k}
the curve
f\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{k}
is homotopic in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
(with fixed endpoints) to a curve in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
.

Consider the arcs
\alpha_{k}
with the following property. For an open arc
\alpha^{\prime}_{k}
in
\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}
which contains the closed arc
\alpha_{k}
the mapping
f\circ\alpha^{\prime}_{k}
is homotopic in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
(with fixed endpoints) to a curve contained in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\mathbb{R}
. Induction on the arcs by inclusion shows that this property is satisfied for all maximal arcs among the
\alpha_{k}
and, hence,
f\mid\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}
is contractible in
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
. Hence, if the claim was not true, then for each hole
\mathcal{C}_{j},\,j\leq m
, whose boundary contains limit points of a connected component of
L
, the monodromy along the curve
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{j}
(with any base point) that represents

e_{j,0}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

would be trivial. Then all monodromies would be trivial, which contradicts the fact that the mapping is not contractible. The contradiction proves the claim.

With
j^{\prime}
and
j^{\prime\prime}
being the numbers of the claim and
j^{\prime}\leq m
we consider the set
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}_{2}
which consists of the following primitive elements:

e_{j^{\prime},0}
, the element
(e_{j^{\prime\prime},0})^{-1}
provided
j^{\prime\prime}\neq m+1
, and
e_{j^{\prime},0}\,e_{j,0}
for all
j=1,\ldots,m,\,j\neq j^{\prime},j\neq j^{\prime\prime}
. The free homotopy class of each element of
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}
has intersection number
1
with
L_{0}
after suitable orientation of the curve
L_{0}
.
Each product of at most two different elements of
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}
is a primitive element of
\pi_{1}(X,q)
and is contained in
\mathcal{E}_{4}
. Moreover, the intersection number with
L_{0}
of the free homotopy class of each product of at most two different elements of
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}

equals
1
or
2
.
Each element of
\mathcal{E}
is the product of at most two elements of
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}\cup(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})^{-1}
.

The proposition is proved for the case of planar domains
X
.

\Box

Proof of Proposition
4
.

1. A torus with a hole.

Consider the curve
L_{0}
and the set
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}\subset\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
obtained in Proposition
3
. For one of the functions
M_{l}\circ f
, denoted by
F
, the image
F(L_{0})
is contained in
(-1,1)
.
Let
e_{0}
,
e_{0}^{\prime}
be the two elements of
\mathcal{E}
.
Move the base point
q_{0}
to a point
q\in L_{0}
along a curve
\alpha
in
X
, and consider the generators
e=\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(e_{0})
and

e^{\prime}=\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}({e^{\prime}_{0}})
of
\pi_{1}(X,q)
, and the set
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})\subset\pi_{1}(X,q)
. Then
e
and
e^{\prime}
are products of at most two elements of
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})
.
Since the free homotopy class of an element of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
coincides with the free homotopy class of the element of
\pi_{1}(X,q)
obtained by applying
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}
,
the free homotopy class of each product of one or two elements of
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})
intersects
L_{0}
.
We may assume as in the proof of Proposition
3
that
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})
consists of the elements
e
and
e^{\prime\prime}
, where
e^{\prime\prime}
is either equal to
{e^{\prime}}^{\pm 1}
, or equals the product of
e
and
e^{\prime}
.
Lemma
4
applies to the pair
e
,
e^{\prime\prime}
,
the function
F
,
and the curve
L_{0}
.
Since
F
is irreducible, the monodromies of
F
along

e
and
e^{\prime\prime}
are not powers of a single standard generator of the fundamental group of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})
.
Hence, the monodromy along each of the
e
and
e^{\prime\prime}
is the product of at most two elements of
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding
2\pi\lambda_{e,e^{\prime\prime}}
. Therefore,
the monodromy of
F
along each of the
e
and
e^{\prime\prime}
has
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding
4\pi\lambda_{e,e^{\prime\prime}}
. Notice that
\lambda_{e,e^{\prime\prime}}=\lambda_{e_{0}e_{0}^{\prime\prime}}\leq\lambda_{3}(X)
, since
e_{0}^{\prime\prime}
is the product of at most two factors, each an element of
\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{E}^{-1}
. Since
e^{\prime}
is the product of at most two different elements among the
e
and
e^{\prime\prime}
and their inverses, we obtain Proposition
4
for
e
and
e^{\prime}
, in particular
\mathcal{L}_{-}(F_{*}(e))
and
\mathcal{L}_{-}(F_{*}(e^{\prime}))
do not exceed
8\pi\lambda_{3}(X)
.
Proposition
4
is proved for tori with a hole.

2. A planar domain.
Consider the curve
L_{0}
and the set
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}
of Proposition
3
.
Move the base point
q_{0}
along an arc
\alpha
to a point
q\in L_{0}
. Then
f(q)\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{-1,1\}
and for one of the mappings
f
,
M_{1}\circ f
, or
M_{2}\circ f
, denoted by
F
, the inclusion
F(L_{0})\subset(-1,1))
holds, hence,
q^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}F(q)
is contained in
(-1,1)
. Denote
e_{j}=\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(e_{j,0})
for each element
e_{j,0}\in\mathcal{E}
. The
e_{j}
form the basis
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E})
of
\pi_{1}(X,q)
. The set
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})
consists of primitive elements of
\pi_{1}(X,q)
such that the free homotopy class of each product of one or two elements of
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})
intersects
L_{0}
. Moreover, each element of
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E})
is the product of one or two elements of
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})\cup(\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}))^{-1}
.

By the condition of the proposition not all monodromies
F_{*}(e),\,e\in\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}),
are (trivial or non-trivial) powers of the same standard generator of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})
.
Apply Lemma
4
to all pairs of elements of
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})
whose monodromies are not (trivial or non-trivial) powers of the same standard generator of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})
.
Since the product of at most two different elements of
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})
is contained in
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{4})
, Lemma
4
shows that the monodromy
F_{*}(e)
along each element
e\in\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})
is the product of at most two factors, each with
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding
2\pi\lambda_{4}(X)
.
Since each element of
\mbox{Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E})
is a product of at most two factors in
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}\cup(\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime})^{-1}
,
the monodromy
F_{*}(e_{j})
along each generator
e_{j}
of
\pi_{1}(X,q)
is the product of at most
4
factors of
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding
2\pi\lambda_{4}(X)
, and, hence, each monodromy
F_{*}(e_{j})
has
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding
8\pi\lambda_{4}(X)
.
Proposition
4
is proved for planar domains.

3.1. The general case. Diagrams of coverings.

We will use diagrams of coverings to reduce this case to the case of a torus with a hole or to the case of the Riemann sphere with three holes.

Let as before
\tilde{q}_{0}
be the point in
\tilde{X}
with
{\sf P}(\tilde{q}_{0})=q_{0}

chosen in Section
2
. Let
N
be a subgroup of the fundamental group
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
and let
\omega^{N}:\tilde{X}\to\tilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(N)=X(N)
be the projection defined in Section
2
. Put
(q_{0})_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\omega^{N}(\tilde{q}_{0})
. For an element
e_{0}\in N\subset\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
we denote by
(e_{0})_{N}
the element of
\pi_{1}(X(N),(q_{0})_{N})
that is obtained as follows. Take a curve
\gamma
in
X
with base point
q_{0}
that represents
e_{0}\in N
. Let

\tilde{\gamma}
be its lift to
\tilde{X}
with terminating point
\tilde{q}_{0}
.
Then
\gamma_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\omega^{N}(\tilde{\gamma})
is a closed curve in
X(N)=\tilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(N)
with base point
(q_{0})_{N}
. The element of
\pi_{1}(X(N),(q_{0})_{N})
represented by
\gamma_{N}
is the required element
(e_{0})_{N}
. All curves
\gamma^{\prime}_{N}
representing
(e_{0})_{N}
have the form
\omega^{N}(\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime})
for a curve
\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime}
in
\tilde{X}
with terminating point
\tilde{q}_{0}
and initial point
({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(e_{0})(\tilde{q}_{0})
.
Since
\omega_{N}\circ\omega^{N}={\sf P}
, the curve
\omega_{N}(\gamma^{\prime}_{N})={\sf P}(\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime})={\gamma}^{\prime}

represents
e_{0}
for each curve
\gamma^{\prime}_{N}
in
X(N)
that represents
(e_{0})_{N}
. We obtain
(\omega_{N})_{*}((e_{0})_{N})=e_{0}
. For two subgroups
N_{1}\leq N_{2}
of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
we obtain
(\omega^{N_{2}}_{N_{1}})_{*}((e_{0})_{N_{1}})=(e_{0})_{N_{2}}
,
e_{0}\in N_{1}
(see the commutative diagram Figure
3
).

Let
\tilde{q}
be another base point of
\tilde{X}
and let
\tilde{\alpha}

be a curve in
\tilde{X}
with initial point
\tilde{q}_{0}
and terminating point
\tilde{q}
. Let again
N
be a subgroup of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
. Put
q_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\omega^{N}(\tilde{q})
.
The curve
\alpha_{N}=\omega^{N}(\tilde{\alpha})
in
X(N)
, and the base point
\tilde{q}
of
\tilde{X}
are compatible, hence,
({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(N)=({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}})^{-1}({\rm Is}_{\alpha_{N}}(N))
and
X({\rm Is}_{\alpha_{N}}(\sigma))=\tilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}})^{-1}({\rm Is}_{\alpha_{N}}(\sigma)),\,\sigma\in N,
is canonically isomorphic to
X(N)=\tilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}(N)
.

We will use the previous notation
\omega^{N_{2}}_{N_{1}}
also for the projection

\tilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}})^{-1}({\rm Is}_{\alpha_{N_{1}}}(N_{1}))\to\tilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}})^{-1}({\rm Is}_{\alpha_{N_{2}}}(N_{2}))
,
N_{1}\leq N_{2}
being subgroups of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
(
N_{1}
may be the identity and
N_{2}
may be
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
.)

Put
\alpha\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}{\sf P}(\tilde{\alpha})
. For an element
e_{0}\in\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
we put
e\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}{\rm Is}_{\alpha}(e_{0})\in\pi_{1}(X,q)
and denote by
e_{N}
the element of
\pi_{1}(X(N),q_{N})
, that is represented by
\omega^{N}(\tilde{\gamma})
for a curve
\tilde{\gamma}
in
\tilde{X}
with terminating point
\tilde{q}
and projection
{\sf P}(\tilde{\gamma})
representing
e
. Again
(\omega^{N_{2}}_{N_{1}})_{*}(e_{N_{1}})=e_{N_{2}}
for subgroups
N_{1}\leq N_{2}
of
\pi_{1}(X,q)
and
e\in N_{1}
, in particular
(\omega_{N})_{*}(e_{N})=e
for a subgroup
N
of
\pi_{1}(X,q)
and
e\in N
.

3.2. The estimate for a chosen pair of monodromies.

Since the mapping
f:X\to\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
is irreducible, there exist two elements
e_{0}^{\prime},\,e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\in\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
such that the monodromies
f_{*}(e_{0}^{\prime})
and
f_{*}(e_{0}^{\prime\prime})
are not powers of a single conjugate of a power of one of the elements
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
or
a_{1}a_{2}
. The fundamental group of the Riemann surface
X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle)
is a free group in the two generators
(e^{\prime}_{0})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
and
(e^{\prime\prime}_{0})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
, hence,
X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle)
is either a torus with a hole or is equal to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with three holes. Moreover, the system
\mathcal{E}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=\{(e^{\prime}_{0})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle},\,(e^{\prime\prime}_{0})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}\}
of generators of the fundamental group
\pi_{1}(X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle),(q_{0})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})
is associated to a standard bouquet of circles for
X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle)
.
This can be seen as follows. The set of generators
\mathcal{E}
of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
is associated to a standard bouquet of circles for
X
. For each
e_{0}\in\mathcal{E}
we denote the circle of the bouquet that represents
e_{0}
by
\gamma_{e_{0}}
.
For each
e_{0}\in\mathcal{E}
we lift the circle
\gamma_{e_{0}}
with base point
q_{0}
to an arc
\widetilde{\gamma_{e_{0}}}
in

\tilde{X}
with terminating point

\tilde{q_{0}}
. Let
D
be a small disc in
X
around
q_{0}
, and
\widetilde{D_{0}}
,
\widetilde{D_{e_{0}}},e_{0}\in\mathcal{E},
be the preimages of
D
under the projection
{\sf P}:\tilde{X}\to X
,
that contain
\tilde{q_{0}}
, or the initial point of
\widetilde{\gamma_{e_{0}}}
, respectively.
We assume that
D
is small enough so that the mentioned preimages of
D
are pairwise disjoint. Put
D_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}=\omega^{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}(\widetilde{D_{0}})
.
For
e_{0}\neq e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}

the image

\omega^{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}(\widetilde{D_{0}}\cup\widetilde{\gamma_{e_{0}}}\cup\widetilde{D_{e_{0}}})

is the union of an arc
\omega^{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}(\widetilde{\gamma_{e_{0}}})
in
X(\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle)
with two disjoint discs, each containing an endpoint of the arc and one of them equal to
D_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
.
For
e_{0}=e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}
the image
\omega^{\langle e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\rangle}(\widetilde{D_{0}}\cup\widetilde{\gamma_{e_{0}}}\cup\widetilde{D_{e_{0}}})
is the union of
D_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
with the loop
(\gamma_{e_{0}})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\omega^{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}(\widetilde{\gamma_{e_{0}}})
. For
e_{0}=e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}
the loop
(\gamma_{e_{0}})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
in
X(\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle)

has base point
(q_{0})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}=\omega^{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}(\tilde{q_{0}})

and represents the generator
(e_{0})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
of the fundamental group of
\pi_{1}(X(\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle),(q_{0})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle})
.
Since
the bouquet of circles
\cup_{e_{0}\in\mathcal{E}}\,\gamma_{e_{0}}
is a standard bouquet of circles for
X
, the union
(\gamma_{e^{\prime}_{0}})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}\cup(\gamma_{e^{\prime\prime}_{0}})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
is a standard bouquet of circles in
X(\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle)
. This can be seen by looking at the intersections of the loops with a circle that is contained in
D_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
and surrounds
(q_{0})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
. By the commutative diagram of coverings the intersection behaviour is the same as for the images of these objects under
\omega_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
.
Hence, since
(\gamma_{e^{\prime}_{0}})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
and
(\gamma_{e^{\prime\prime}_{0}})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
represent the generators
({e^{\prime}_{0}})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
and
({e^{\prime\prime}_{0}})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
of
\mathcal{E}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
, the
union
(\gamma_{e^{\prime}_{0}})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}\cup(\gamma_{e^{\prime\prime}_{0}})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
is a standard bouquet of circles for
X(\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle)
.

The set
X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle)
is either a torus with a hole or is equal to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with three holes.
Apply Proposition
3
to the Riemann surface
X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle)
with base point
(q_{0})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle}
, the holomorphic mapping
f_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=f\circ\omega_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
into
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\}
, and the set of generators
\mathcal{E}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
of the fundamental group
\pi_{1}(X(\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle),(q_{0})_{\langle e^{\prime}_{0},e^{\prime\prime}_{0}\rangle})
. We obtain a relatively closed curve
L_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
on which the function
f_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
is real,
and a set
(\mathcal{E}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})_{2}^{\prime}=\{({\sf e}_{0}^{\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle},({\sf e}_{0}^{\prime\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}\}
which contains one of the elements of
\mathcal{E}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
. The second element of
(\mathcal{E}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})_{2}^{\prime}
is either equal to second element of
\mathcal{E}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
or to its inverse, or to the product of the two elements (in any order) of
\mathcal{E}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
. (We will usually refer to the product
({e}_{0}^{\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}\,({e}_{0}^{\prime\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=({e}_{0}^{\prime}\,{e}_{0}^{\prime\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
,
but we may change the product
({e}_{0}^{\prime}\,{e}_{0}^{\prime\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
to the product
({e}_{0}^{\prime\prime}\,{e}_{0}^{\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
, without changing the arguments and the estimate of the
\mathcal{L}_{-}
of the monodromies of the elements of
\mathcal{E}_{2}^{\prime}
.)
The free homotopy classes

({\sf e}_{0}^{\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

,

({\sf e}_{0}^{\prime\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

, and
\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-44.79669pt\hbox{$({\sf e}_{0}^{\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}\,({\sf e}_{0}^{\prime\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}$}\hskip-44.79669pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.51794pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1037.33662pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{43810652}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1037.33662pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{43810652}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-190.99869pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{43810652}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-190.99928pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{43810652}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}=\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-27.43654pt\hbox{$({\sf e}_{0}^{\prime}\,{\sf e}_{0}^{\prime\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}$}\hskip-27.43654pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.51794pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1037.33662pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{43810652}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1037.33662pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{43810652}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-190.99869pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{43810652}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-190.99928pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{43810652}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}
intersect
L_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
.

Choose a point
q_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}\in L_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
and a point
\tilde{q}\in\tilde{X}
with
\omega^{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}(\tilde{q})=q_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
. Let
\tilde{\alpha}
be a curve in
\tilde{X}
with initial point
\tilde{q}_{0}
and terminating point
\tilde{q}
, and
\alpha_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=\omega^{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}(\tilde{\alpha})
. Put
{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}={\rm Is}_{\alpha_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}}(({\sf e}_{0}^{\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})
and
{\sf e}^{\prime\prime}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}={\rm Is}_{\alpha_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}}(({\sf e}_{0}^{\prime\prime})_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})
. For one out of three Möbius transformations
M_{l}
the mapping
F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=M_{l}\circ f_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}=M_{l}\circ f\circ\omega_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
takes
L_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
to
(-1,1)
, and hence
F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}

takes a value
q^{\prime}=F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}(q_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})\in(-1,1)
at
q_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}
. By Lemma
4
each of the
(F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})
and
(F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime\prime}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})
is the product of at most two elements of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})
of
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding
2\pi\lambda_{3}(X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle))
, hence,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{-}

\displaystyle((F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}))\leq 4\pi\lambda_{3}(X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{-}

\displaystyle((F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime\prime}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}))\leq 4\pi\lambda_{3}(X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle))\,.

It follows that each of the
(F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})_{*}({e}^{\prime}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})
and
(F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})_{*}({e}^{\prime\prime}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})
is the product of at most four elements of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})
of
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding
2\pi\lambda_{3}(X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle))
, hence,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{-}

\displaystyle((F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})_{*}({e}^{\prime}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}))\leq 8\pi\lambda_{3}(X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle)),

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{-}

\displaystyle((F_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})_{*}({e}^{\prime\prime}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle}))\leq 8\pi\lambda_{3}(X(\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle))\,.

It remains to take into account that for a subgroup
N
of
\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})

the equation
(F_{N})_{*}({{e}}_{\langle e_{0}^{\prime},e_{0}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})=F_{*}({e})
holds for each
{e}\in{\rm Is}_{\alpha}(N)
, and

\lambda_{j}(X(N))\leq\lambda_{j}(X)
for each natural number
j
.

3.3. Other generators. Intersection of free homotopy classes with a component of the zero set.

Take any element
e\in{\rm Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E})
that is not in
\langle e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\rangle
.
Then the monodromy
F_{*}(e)
is either equal to the identity, or
one of the pairs
(F_{*}(e),F_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime}))
or
(F_{*}(e),F_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime\prime}))
consists of two elements of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})
that are not powers of the same standard generator
a_{j}
,
j=1
or
2
. Interchanging if necessary
{\sf e}^{\prime}
and
{\sf e}^{\prime\prime}
, we may suppose this option holds for the pair

(F_{*}(e),F_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime}))
. Moreover, changing if necessary
{\sf e}^{\prime}
to its inverse
({\sf e}^{\prime})^{-1}
, we may assume that
{\sf e}^{\prime}
is either an element of
{\rm Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E})
or it is a product of two elements of
{\rm Is}_{\alpha}(\mathcal{E})
. The quotient
X(\langle e,{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle)=\tilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}})^{-1}(\langle e,{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle)
is a Riemann surface whose fundamental group is a free group in two generators. Hence
X(\langle e,{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle)
is either a torus with a hole or is equal to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with three holes.

We consider a diagram of coverings as follows. Let first
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle)=\tilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}})^{-1}(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle)
be the annulus
with base point
q_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}=\omega^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}(\tilde{q})
, that admits a mapping
\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}:X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle)\to X
that represents
{\sf e}^{\prime}
. By Lemma
3
the connected component
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
of
(\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},{\sf e}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})^{-1}(L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},{\sf e}^{\prime\prime}\rangle})
that contains
q_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}=\omega^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}(\tilde{q})
is a relatively closed curve in
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle)
with limit points on both boundary components. The free homotopy class of the generator
{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
of
\pi_{1}(X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle),q_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})
intersects
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
. The mapping
F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}=M_{l}\circ f\circ\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
maps
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
into
(-1,1)
, and
F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}(q_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})=F\circ{\sf P}(\tilde{q})=q^{\prime}
.

Next we consider the quotient
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)=\tilde{X}\diagup({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}})^{-1}(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
whose fundamental group is again a free group in two generators. The image
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}(L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})
is a connected component of the preimage of
(-1,1)
under
F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
. Indeed,
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
is connected as image of a connected set under a continuous mapping, and
F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}(\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}(L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}))=F\circ\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\circ\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}(L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})=F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}(L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})\subset(-1,1)
. Moreover, since the mapping
\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}:X({\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})\to X({\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})
is a covering, its restriction
({\rm Im}F\circ\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})^{-1}(0)\to({\rm Im}F\circ\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})^{-1}(0)
is a covering. Hence the image under
\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
of a connected component of
({\rm Im}F\circ\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})^{-1}(0)

is open and closed in
({\rm Im}F\circ\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})^{-1}(0)
. Hence,
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}(L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})
is a connected component of the preimage of
(-1,1)
under
F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
.
Put
q_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}=\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}(q_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})=\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\circ\omega^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}(\tilde{q})=\omega^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}(\tilde{q})
. Note that

F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}(q_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})=F\circ\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}(q_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})=F(q)=q^{\prime}
.

The free homotopy class

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

in
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)

that is related to
{\sf e}^{\prime}
intersects
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
. Indeed,
consider any loop
\gamma^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
in
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
with some base point
q^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
, that represents

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

.
There exists a loop
{\gamma}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
in
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle)
which represents

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

such that
\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}({\gamma}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})=\gamma^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
.
Such a curve
{\gamma}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
can be obtained as follows. There is a loop
\gamma^{\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
in
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
with base point
{q}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
that represents
({\sf e}^{\prime})_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
, and a curve
\alpha^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
in
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
, such that
\gamma^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
is homotopic with fixed endpoint to
(\alpha^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})^{-1}\,\gamma^{\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\,\alpha^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
. Consider the lift
\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}
of
\gamma^{\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
to
\tilde{X}
with terminating point
\tilde{q}
, and the lift
\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}
of
\alpha^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
with initial point
\tilde{q}
. The initial point of
\gamma^{\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
equals
\sigma(\tilde{q})
for the covering transformation
\sigma=({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}})^{-1}({\sf e}^{\prime})=({\rm Is}^{\tilde{q}_{0}})^{-1}({\sf e}^{\prime}_{0})
. (See equation (
6
).)
The initial point of the curve
\sigma((\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime})^{-1})\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}
is obtained from its terminating point by applying the covering transformation
\sigma
. Hence,

\omega^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}(\sigma((\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime})^{-1})\tilde{\gamma}^{\prime\prime}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime})
is a closed curve in
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle)
that represents

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

and projects to
(\alpha^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})^{-1}\,\gamma^{\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\,\alpha^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
under
\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
.
Since
\gamma^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
is homotopic to
(\alpha^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})^{-1}\,\gamma^{\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\,\alpha^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
with fixed base point, it also has a lift to
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle)
which represents

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

.

Since

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

intersects
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
,
the loop

{\gamma}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
has an intersection point
p^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
with
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}
. The point
p^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}=\omega_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle}^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}(p^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime}\rangle})
is contained in
\gamma^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
and in
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
.
We proved that the free homotopy class

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

in
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
intersects
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
.

3.4. A system of generators associated to a standard bouquet of circles.

We claim that
the system of generators
\;{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle},\;\;e_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\;
of
\;\pi_{1}(X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle),q_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})
is associated to a standard bouquet of circles for
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
. If
{\sf e}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{E}
the claim can be obtained as in paragraph 3.2. Suppose
{\sf e}^{\prime}=e^{\prime}e^{\prime\prime}
for
e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{E}
. Consider the system
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
of generators of
\pi_{1}(X,q)
that is obtained from
\mathcal{E}
by replacing
e^{\prime}
by
e^{\prime}e^{\prime\prime}
.
If
e^{\prime}
and
e^{\prime\prime}
correspond to a handle of
X
, then
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}

is also associated to a standard bouquet of circles for
X
, see
Figure 4a for the case when
e^{\prime}
is represented by an
\alpha
-curve and
e^{\prime\prime}
is represented by a
\beta
-curve. The situation when
e^{\prime}
is represented by a
\beta
-curve and
e^{\prime\prime}
is represented by an
\alpha
-curve is similar. The claim is obtained as in paragraph 3.2.

Figure 4.
Standard bouquets of circles

Suppose one of the pairs
(e,e^{\prime})
or
(e,e^{\prime\prime})
corresponds to a handle of
X
.
We assume that
e
corresponds to an
\alpha
-curve and
e^{\prime}
corresponds to a
\beta
-curve
of a handle of
X

(see Figure 4b). The remaining cases are treated similarly, maybe, after replacing
e^{\prime}e^{\prime\prime}
by
e^{\prime\prime}e^{\prime}
(see paragraph 3.2). With our assumption
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
is associated to a bouquet of circles that is a deformation retract for
X
, but it is not a standard bouquet of circles. Nevertheless, the pair
(e_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle},{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})
with
{\sf e}^{\prime}=e^{\prime}e^{\prime\prime}
is associated to a standard bouquet of circles for
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
. This can be seen as before. Consider the bouquet of circles corresponding to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
and take its union with a disc
D
around
q
. Lift this set to
\tilde{X}
. We obtain
the union of a collection of arcs in
\tilde{X}
with terminating point
\tilde{q}
, with a collection of discs in
\tilde{X}
around
\tilde{q}
and around the initial points of the arcs. Take the union of the arcs and the discs. The image in
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
of this union under the projection
\omega^{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
is the union of the two loops
(\gamma_{e})_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\cup(\gamma_{{\sf e}^{\prime}})_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
, the disc
D_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
and a set, that is contractible to
D_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
. Looking at the intersection of the two loops with a small circle contained in
D_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
and surrounding
q_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
, we see as before that

(\gamma_{e})_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\cup(\gamma_{{\sf e^{\prime}}})_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
is a standard bouquet of circles for
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
. In this case
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
is a torus with a hole.

In the remaining case no pair of generators among
e
,
e^{\prime}
, and
e^{\prime\prime}
corresponds to a handle.
In this case again
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
does not correspond to a standard bouquet of circles for
X
. But
\{e_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle},(e^{\prime}e^{\prime\prime})_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\}
(maybe, after changing
e^{\prime}e^{\prime\prime}
to
e^{\prime\prime}e^{\prime}
)
corresponds to a standard bouquet of circles for
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
. (See Figure 4c for the case when walking along a small circle around
q
counterclockwise, we meet the incoming and outgoing rays of representatives of the three elements of
\mathcal{E}
in the order
e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}
. If the order is different the situation is similar, maybe, after replacing
e^{\prime}e^{\prime\prime}
by
e^{\prime\prime}e^{\prime}
.) In this case
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
is a planar domain.

3.5. End of the proof.

Consider first the case when
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
is a torus with a hole.
Since

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

intersects
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
, we see as in the proof when
X
itself is a torus with a hole, that the curve
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
cannot be contractible or contractible to the hole, and
the intersection number must be different from zero. Then the intersection number with
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
of the free homotopy class of one the choices
e_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}^{\pm 1}
or
({\sf e}^{\prime}e)_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
, denoted by
{\sf e}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
, is not zero and has the same sign. By lemma
4
each of the

(F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})
and
(F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})
is the product of at most two elements of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})

with
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding

(18)

2\pi\lambda_{{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle},{\sf e}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}}\leq 2\pi\lambda_{5}(X),

since
{\sf e}^{\prime}
is the product of at most two elements of
\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{E}^{-1}
and
{\sf e}^{\prime\prime\prime}
is the product of at most three elements of
\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{E}^{-1}
.
The element
e
is the product of at most two different elements among the
{\sf e}^{\prime}
and
{\sf e}^{\prime\prime\prime}
or their inverses. Hence, the monodromy
F_{*}(e)=(F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})_{*}(e_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})
is the product of at most four elements with
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding (
18
).
Hence,

(19)

F_{*}(e)\leq 8\pi\lambda_{5}(X)\,.

Consider now the case when
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
equals
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with three holes. Since
{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
and
e_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
correspond to a standard bouquet of circles for
X(\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle)
, the curves representing
{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
surround counterclockwise one of the holes, denoted by
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, and the curves representing
e_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
surround counterclockwise another hole, denoted by
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
.
After applying a Möbius transformation we may assume that the remaining hole, denoted by
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}
, contains the point
\infty
.
There are several possibilities for the behaviour of the curve
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
.
Since

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

intersects
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
, the curve
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
must have limit points on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
. The first possibility is that
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
has limit points on
\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and
\partial\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
, the second possibility is,
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}

has limit points on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\infty}
, the third possibility is,
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
has all limit points on
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
is contained in the bounded connected component of
\mathbb{C}\setminus(L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}\cup\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
.

In the first case the free homotopy classes

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

and

e_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}^{-1}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

have positive intersection number with the suitably oriented curve
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
. In the second case the free homotopy classes

{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

and

({{\sf e}^{\prime}e})_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}

\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt

have positive intersection number with the suitably oriented curve
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
. In the third case the free homotopy classes of
{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
,
({{\sf e}^{\prime}}^{2}e)_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
and of their product intersect
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
. The first two cases were treated in paragraph 2 of this section.
The statement concerning the third case is proved as follows.

Any curve that is contained in the complement of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\cup L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
has either winding number zero around
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
(as a curve in the complex plane
\mathbb{C}
), or its winding number around
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
coincides with the winding number around
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
.
On the other hand the representatives of the free homotopy class of
{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}

have winding number
1
around
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and winding number
0
around
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
.
The representatives of the free homotopy class of
({{\sf e}^{\prime}}^{2}e)_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
have winding number
2
around
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, and winding number
1
around
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
.
By the same argument the free homotopy class of the product of
{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
and
({{\sf e}^{\prime}}^{2}e)_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
intersects
L_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
.

We let
{\sf e}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
be equal to
e_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}^{-1}
in the first case, equal to
({{\sf e}^{\prime}e})_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
in the second case, and equal to
({{\sf e}^{\prime}}^{2}e)_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}
in the third case.

By lemma
4
each of the

(F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})
and
(F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})_{*}({\sf e}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})
is the product of at most two elements of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{C}\setminus\{-1,1\},q^{\prime})

with
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding

(20)

2\pi\lambda_{{\sf e}^{\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle},{\sf e}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle}}\leq 2\pi\lambda_{7}(X),

We used that
{\sf e}^{\prime}
is the product of at most two elements of
\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{E}^{-1}
,
e\in\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{E}^{-1}
and
{\sf e}^{\prime\prime\prime}
is the product of at most five elements of
\mathcal{E}\cup\mathcal{E}^{-1}
.
Since
e
is the product of at most two different elements among the
({\sf e}^{\prime})^{\pm 1}
and
({\sf e}^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\pm 1}
, the monodromy
F_{*}(e)=(F_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})_{*}(e_{\langle{\sf e}^{\prime},e\rangle})
is the product of at most four elements with
\mathcal{L}_{-}
not exceeding (
20
).
Hence,

(21)

F_{*}(e)\leq 8\pi\lambda_{7}(X)\,.

The proposition is proved.

\Box

4.
(
\sf{g},\sf{m}
)-bundles over Riemann surfaces

We will consider bundles whose fibers are smooth surfaces or Riemann surfaces of type

(\sf{g},\sf{m})
.

Definition 2
.

(Smooth oriented
(\textsf{g},\textsf{m})
fiber bundles.)

Let
X
be a smooth oriented manifold of dimension
k
, let

{\mathcal{X}}
be a smooth (oriented)
manifold of dimension
k+2
and
{\mathcal{P}}:{\mathcal{X}}\to X

an orientation preserving smooth proper submersion such that for each point
x\in X
the
fiber
{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}(x)
is a closed oriented surface of genus
\sf{g}
.
Let
\mathbold{E}
be a smooth submanifold of
\mathcal{X}
that intersects each fiber
\mathcal{P}^{-1}(x)
along a set
E_{x}
of
\sf{m}
distinguished points.
Then the tuple
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{g},\textsf{m}}=({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{P}},\mathbold{E},X)
is called
a smooth (oriented) fiber bundle over
X
with fiber a smooth closed oriented surface of
genus
g
with
m
distinguished points
(for short, a smooth oriented
(\textsf{g},\textsf{m})
-bundle).

If
{\sf m}=0
the set
\mathbold E
is the empty set and we will often denote the bundle
by
({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{P}},X)
.
If
{\sf{m}}>0
the mapping
x\to E_{x}
locally defines
\sf m
smooth sections.

(\textsf{g},0)
-bundles will also be called genus
g
fiber bundles.
For
\textsf{g}=1
and
\textsf{m}=0
the bundle is also called an elliptic fiber
bundle.

In the case when the base manifold is a Riemann surface, a
holomorphic
(\textsf{g}
,
\textsf{m})
fiber bundle over
X

is defined as follows.

Definition 3
.

Let
X
be a Riemann surface, let
{\mathcal{X}}
be a complex surface, and
{\mathcal{P}}

a holomorphic proper submersion from
{\mathcal{X}}
onto
X
, such that
each fiber
\mathcal{P}^{-1}(x)
is a closed Riemann surface of genus
\sf g
.
Suppose
\mathbold{E}
is a complex one-dimensional submanifold of
\mathcal{X}
that intersects each fiber
\mathcal{P}^{-1}(x)
along a
set
E_{x}
of
{\sf m}
distinguished points. Then the
tuple
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{g},\textsf{m}}=({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{P}},\mathbold{E},X)
is called a
holomorphic
(\textsf{g}
,
\textsf{m})
fiber bundle over
X
.

Notice that for
{\sf{m}}>0
the mapping
x\to E_{x}

locally defines
\sf{m}
holomorphic sections.

Two smooth oriented (holomorphic, respectively)
(\textsf{g},\,\textsf{m})
fiber
bundles,
{\mathfrak{F}}^{0}=({\mathcal{X}}^{0},{\mathcal{P}}^{0},\mathbold{E}^{0},X)
and
{\mathfrak{F}}^{1}=({\mathcal{X}}^{1},{\mathcal{P}}^{1},\mathbold{E}^{1},X)
, are called smoothly isomorphic (holomorphically isomorphic, respectively,) if there are smooth (holomorphic,
respectively) homeomorphisms
\Phi:\mathcal{X}^{0}\to\mathcal{X}^{1}
and
\phi:X^{0}\to X^{1}

such that for each
x\in X^{0}
the mapping
\Phi
maps the fiber
(\mathcal{P}^{0})^{-1}(x)

onto the fiber
(\mathcal{P}^{1})^{-1}(\phi(x))
and the set of distinguished points in

(\mathcal{P}^{0})^{-1}(x)
to the set of distinguished points in
(\mathcal{P}^{1})^{-1}(\phi(x))
.
Holomorphically isomorphic bundles will be considered the same holomorphic bundles.

Two smooth (oriented)
(\textsf{g},\textsf{m})
fiber bundles
over the same oriented
smooth base manifold
X
,
{\mathfrak{F}}^{0}=({\mathcal{X}}^{0},{\mathcal{P}}^{0},\mathbold{E}^{0},X)
, and
{\mathfrak{F}}^{1}=({\mathcal{X}}^{1},{\mathcal{P}}^{1},\mathbold{E}^{1},X)
, are called (free) isotopic if for an open interval
I
containing
[0,1]
there
is a smooth
(\textsf{g},\textsf{m})
fiber bundle
({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{P}},\mathbold{E},X\times I)
over the base
X\times I
(called an isotopy) with the following property. For each
t\in[0,1]
we put

{\mathcal{Y}}^{t}={\mathcal{P}}^{-1}(X\times\{t\})
and
\mathbold{E}^{t}=\mathbold{E}\cap{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}(X\times\{t\})
. The bundle
{\mathfrak{F}}^{0}\,
is
equal to

\,\left({\mathcal{Y}}^{0},{\mathcal{P}}\mid{\mathcal{Y}}^{0},\mathbold{E}^{0},X\times\{0\}\right)\,
, and the bundle
\,{\mathfrak{F}}^{1}\,
is
equal to
\,\bigl({\mathcal{Y}}^{1},{\mathcal{P}}\mid{\mathcal{Y}}^{1},\mathbold{E}^{1},X\times\{1\}\bigl)\,
.

Two smooth
({\sf g},{\sf m})
-bundles are smoothly isomorphic if and only if they are isotopic (see
[
20
]
).

Denote
by
S
a reference surface of genus
\sf g
with a set
E\subset S
of
\sf{m}
distinguished points.
By Ehresmann’s Fibration Theorem each smooth
(\sf{g},\sf{m})
-bundle
{\mathfrak{F}}_{\textsf{g},\textsf{m}}=({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{P}},\mathbold{E},X)
with set of distinguished points
E_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle def}}{{=}}\mathbold{E}\cap{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}(x)
in the fiber over
x
is
locally smoothly trivial, i.e. each point in
X
has a neighbourhood
U\subset X

such that the restriction of the bundle to
U
is isomorphic to the trivial bundle
\big(U\times S,{\rm{pr}}_{1},U\times E,U\big)
with set
\{x\}\times E
of distinguished points in the fiber
\{x\}\times S
over
x
. Here
{\rm pr}_{1}:U\times S\to U
is the projection onto the first factor.

The idea of the proof of Ehresmann’s Theorem is the following. Choose smooth coordinates on
U

by a mapping from a rectangular box to
U
.
Consider
smooth vector fields
v_{j}
on
U
, which form a basis of the tangent
space at each point of
U
. Take smooth vector fields
V_{j}
on
{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}(U)
that are tangent to
\mathbold{E}
at points of this set and are mapped to

v_{j}
by the differential of

{\mathcal{P}}
. Such vector fields can easily be obtained locally.
To obtain the globally defined vector fields
V_{j}
on
{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}(U)

one uses partitions of unity. The required diffeomorphism
\varphi_{U}
is
obtained by composing the flows of these vector fields (in any fixed
order).

In this way a trivialization of the bundle can be obtained over any simply connected domain.

Let
q_{0}
be a base point in
X
and
\gamma(t),\,t\in[0,1],
a smooth curve in
X
with
base point
q_{0}
that represents an element
e
of the fundamental group

\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
. Let
\varphi^{t}:\mathcal{P}^{-1}(q_{0})\to\mathcal{P}^{-1}(\gamma(t)),\,t\in[0,1]
,
\varphi^{0}=\mbox{Id}
, be a smooth family
of diffeomorphisms that map the set of distinguished points in
\mathcal{P}^{-1}(q_{0})
to
the set of distinguishes points in
\mathcal{P}^{-1}(\gamma(t))
. To obtain such a family we may restrict the bundle to the closed curve given by
\gamma
and lift the restriction to a bundle over the real axis
\mathbb{R}
. The family of diffeomorphisms may be obtained by considering Ehrenpreis’ vector field for the lifted bundle and take the flow of this vector field.
The mapping

\varphi^{1}
obtained for
t=1
is an orientation preserving self-homeomorphism of the fiber over
q_{0}
that
preserves the set of distinguished points. Its isotopy class depends only on the homotopy
class of the curve and the isotopy class of the bundle. The isotopy class of its inverse

(\varphi^{1})^{-1}
is called the monodromy of the bundle along
e
. Assign to each element
e\in\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
the monodromy of the bundle along
e
. We obtain a homomorphism from

\pi_{1}(X,q_{0})
to the group of isotopy classes of
